Experimental Reference Value & Rejection of Client Amendment\n* **Adopted Experimental Reference Value:** **$D = 118\\text{ min}$**, certified in Red Butte test report `RBFMT-25-0918` \u00a75.3 and Ironwood certification memorandum `IWFPE-CERT-25-0374` \u00a72 (governed by thermocouple TC-6 over a continuous 3.8 mm steel tie course).\n* **Disposition on Bianca Rennert's 129 min Figure:** **Formally rejected / disallowed** for validation under `CFDME_Validation_Procedure_rev2.pdf` \u00a76. Substituting client-proposed post-test amendments requires: (1) a formal reissued laboratory test report from Red Butte (e.g., `RBFMT-25-0918-R1`) signed by authorized lab signatory Glen Whitfield, (2) a NIST-traceable calibration certificate covering channel TC-6, and (3) an explicit supersession statement. None exist; Red Butte's certified value of $D = 118\\text{ min}$ stands as the sole admissible physical reference.\n\n#### 4. ASME V&V 20 Validation Rollup & Uncertainty Analysis\n* **Mandatory Dedicated Rollup Line (\u00a73):**\n  $$\\mathbf{S = 131\\text{ min},\\quad D = 118\\text{ min},\\quad E = +13\\text{ min},\\quad u_{\\text{val}} = \\pm 9\\text{ min}}$$\n  *(Citations: $S = 131\\text{ min}$ from companion run `E119_companion_sim_results.csv`; $D = 118\\text{ min}$ from Red Butte report `RBFMT-25-0918` \u00a75.3 / Ironwood memo `IWFPE-CERT-25-0374` \u00a72).*\n* **Comparison Error:** $E = S - D = 131\\text{ min} - 118\\text{ min} = \\mathbf{+13\\text{ min}}$ (positive sign explicitly retained, denoting that the simulation over-predicts endurance).\n* **Validation Uncertainty:** $u_{\\text{val}} = \\sqrt{u_{\\text{num}}^2 + u_{\\text{inp}}^2 + u_{\\text{exp}}^2} = \\sqrt{(4\\text{ min})^2 + (6\\text{ min})^2 + (5\\text{ min})^2} = \\sqrt{77\\text{ min}^2} \\approx 8.775\\text{ min} \\rightarrow \\mathbf{\\pm 9\\text{ min}}$.\n* **Acceptance Criterion Evaluation:** The firm acceptance threshold is $|E| \\le 10\\% \\text{ of } D = \\mathbf{11.8\\text{ min}}$. Because $|E| = 13\\text{ min} > 11.8\\text{ min}$, the companion simulation **FAILS the validation acceptance criterion**.\n* **Comparison Against Validation Uncertainty:** Because $|E| = 13\\text{ min} > 9\\text{ min}\\ (u_{\\text{val}})$, the discrepancy exceeds the validation noise floor and is resolved as a **statistically significant, actionable model error**.\n* **Largest Passing Prediction:** The maximum simulation prediction that would have cleared the 10% criterion against $D = 118\\text{ min}$ is $S_{\\max} = D + 11.8\\text{ min} = \\mathbf{129.8\\text{ min}}$ (or **$129\\text{ min}$** to the nearest whole minute).\n\n#### 5. Model Diagnostics & Thermal Physics\n* **Comparison 1 (Criteria Separation):** The elapsed time separating single-point failure from average-rise failure is exactly **45 min in the physical test** (163 min \u2212 118 min) and exactly **45 min in the simulation** (176 min \u2212 131 min).\n* **Comparison 2 (Governing Point Elevation Above Mean):** At governing failure, the peak temperature sits **85 \u00b0C above the 9-thermocouple mean in the physical test** (181 \u00b0C rise vs. 96 \u00b0C rise) and **91.2 \u00b0C above the mean in the simulation** (202.1 \u00b0C vs. 110.9 \u00b0C at 131 min), agreeing within 6.2 \u00b0C.\n* **Physical Diagnosis:** Preserving the 45 min separation and closely matching tie thermal spike confirms that metallic conduction and multi-region conjugate thermal bridging along the 3.8 mm steel ties are accurately captured. The uniform +13 min shift across both criteria indicates a **bulk through-wall thermal conduction lag**.\n* **Allocation of Rework:** Rework belongs in the bulk solid thermophysical properties of the 3D-printed concrete (temperature-dependent thermal conductivity $k$, density $\\rho$, specific heat $c_p$) and/or cavity radiation/convection transport (emissivity, cavity coupling), **not** in tie geometry or tie discretization.\n* **Rework Status:** As of October 10, 2025 (10/10/2025), **no rework has been started, queued, or executed**.\n\n#### 6. Implications for Phase 1 Deliverables\n* Phase 1 Report rev B cannot claim validation; Section 7 must be populated with a formal, documented finding of non-validation ($E = +13\\text{ min}$ exceeding the 11.8 min threshold).\n* Through-wall thermal predictions across the SOW \u00a73.1 wildfire matrix remain uncalibrated and **unconservative** (heat will penetrate the real wall faster than simulated).\n* Final validation of the wall model is deferred to concrete thermal property recalibration or the client-commissioned ASTM E119 confirmatory test window at Red Butte scheduled for October 27, 2025 (10/27/2025), ahead of final Phase 1 delivery on November 7, 2025 (11/07/2025).\n\n#### 7. Scope Limitations Regarding Ocotillo's Wildfire Claim\n* ASTM E119 is a standardized, comparative post-flashover compartment furnace rating. It **does not validate** Ocotillo's marketing claim that printed-concrete envelopes outperform wood-frame construction in a Sonoran Desert buffelgrass WUI wildfire event.\n* E119 endurance cannot be extrapolated to wildfire survival time due to fundamental differences in exposure dynamics (transient 2 min to 5 min flame-front passage vs. monotonically rising furnace curve; high-velocity boundary-layer winds and flame tilt vs. quiescent furnace; ember/firebrand attack vs. clean gas combustion).\n* Closing the empirical gap requires physical testing under representative wind-driven WUI wildfire exposure conditions. Any future comparison must follow the ASME V&V 20 format ($E = S - D \\pm u_{\\text{val}}$ against a quantitative criterion) and **never** a binary yes/no.\n\n#### 8. Fire-Resistance Rating Impact, Authority, and Stamp Language\n* **Rating Impact if Substantiated:** If Bianca's 129 min figure were ever officially substantiated by a reissued Red Butte report, the demonstrated endurance would exceed the 120 min rung, qualifying the assembly for an **upgraded 2-hour (120 min) fire-resistance rating** (up from its current 1-hour / 60 min rating).\n* **Exclusive Authority:** Sole authority to issue, amend, or restate the fire-resistance rating rests with **Ironwood Fire Protection Engineering (Marla Kettering, PE, AZ PE #45106)** upon receipt of an amended lab report from Red Butte. The CFD ME provides thermal analysis only and has no legal authority to certify ratings.\n* **Mandatory Professional Stamp Disclaimer:**\n  `\"CFD thermal analysis only. Fire-resistance rating certified separately by others.\"`\n\n#### 9. The companion run of record predicts a governing single-point endurance of 131 min, whereas certified Red Butte test report RBFMT-25-0918 establishes a measured endurance of 118 min, resulting in a comparison error of E = +13 min. This discrepancy exceeds our firm acceptance threshold of 10 % of D (11.8 min) as well as the combined validation uncertainty of \u00b19 min. Under CFDME Validation Procedure rev 2 \u00a76, your proposed post-test calibration revision to 129 min cannot be adopted absent an officially reissued laboratory test report and NIST-traceable calibration certificate from Red Butte Fire & Materials Testing. Furthermore, the certified fire-resistance rating of the assembly remains 1-hour (60 min) under the exclusive professional authority of Ironwood Fire Protection Engineering (Marla Kettering, PE, AZ PE #45106); The CFD ME provides computational thermal analysis only and is not legally empowered to certify or alter fire ratings.
